```
1	---
2	name: skf-test-skill
3	description: Cognitive completeness verification — quality gate before export. Use when the user requests to "test a skill" or "verify skill completeness."
4	---
5	
6	# Test Skill
7	
8	## Overview
9	
10	Verifies that a skill is complete enough to be useful to an AI agent by checking coverage of the public API surface (naive mode) or validating SKILL.md + references coherence (contextual mode). Produces a completeness score and gap report as a quality gate before export. Every finding must trace to actual code with file:line citations.
11	
12	## Conventions
13	
14	- Bare paths (e.g. `references/<name>.md`) resolve from the skill root.
15	- `references/` holds prompt content carved out of SKILL.md (workflow stages chained via frontmatter `nextStepFile`, plus static reference docs); `scripts/` and `assets/` hold deterministic helpers and templates.
16	- `{skill-root}` resolves to this skill's installed directory (where `customize.toml` lives, if present).
17	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
18	- `{skill-name}` resolves to the skill directory's basename.
19	
20	## Role
21	
22	You are a skill auditor and completeness analyst operating in Ferris's Audit mode. This is a deterministic quality gate — you bring AST-backed analysis expertise and zero-hallucination verification, while the skill artifacts provide the evidence.
23	
24	## Workflow Rules
25	
26	These rules apply to every step in this workflow:
27	
28	- Zero hallucination — every finding must trace to actual code with file:line citations
29	- Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps
30	- Update `stepsCompleted` in output file frontmatter before loading next step
31	- Always communicate in `{communication_language}`
32	- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision
33	
34	## Stages
35	
36	| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
37	|---|------|------|--------------|
38	| 1 | Initialize & Load Skill | references/init.md | Yes |
39	| 2 | Detect Mode | references/detect-mode.md | Yes |
40	| 3 | Coverage Check | references/coverage-check.md | Yes |
```

id: toolu_01BFDvXrz1uVtmJWDfi3hZ42
```json
{
  "command": "python3 <<'PYEOF'\nimport re\nimport pathlib\n\n# Match the **CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence... line (3 variants) plus the blank line after it where present\npattern = re.compile(r'\\*\\*CRITICAL:\\*\\* Follow this sequence exactly\\.[^\\n]*\\n(\\n)?', re.MULTILINE)\n\ncount_total = 0\ncount_files = 0\nfor f in pathlib.Path('src').rglob('references/*.md'):\n    if '.analysis' in str(f):\n        continue\n    content = f.read_text(encoding='utf-8')\n    matches = pattern.findall(content)\n    if matches:\n        new_content = pattern.sub('', content)\n        f.write_text(new_content, encoding='utf-8')\n        count_total += len(matches)\n        count_files += 1\n        print(f\"  {f}: {len(matches)} removed\")\n\nprint(f\"\\nTotal: {count_total} CRITICAL banner lines removed across {count_files} files\")\nPYEOF",
  "description": "Drop CRITICAL banner body line from step files"
}
```

## What was avoided

- Did NOT touch `Auto-Proceed` inline content (load-bearing behavioral instruction)

Net: the original framing in #335 of "drop the trio and three banner types" was outdated. The per-rule lens propagates the v2 setup quality decision uniformly without regression.

> AGENT

**PR #336 opened: https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/336**

## What landed

- **Commit** `08c01c98` on branch `chore/skf-issue-335-drop-redundant-scaffolding`
- **82 files changed, 2 insertions, 162 deletions**
- Pre-commit hooks passed full validation: 1346 Python tests, schema + reference checks, ESLint, markdownlint (224 .md), Prettier

## What was avoided (regression prevention)

The audit caught that the v2 skf-setup quality pass (commit `3dd864cd`) made a deliberate **per-rule** decision — keep Rule 3, drop Rules 1+2. The original #335 framing of "drop the whole trio" would have undone that. The corrected plan:
- Propagates the v2 per-rule trim module-wide
- Surfaces and fixes a real drift (`skf-quick-skill` missing Rule 3)
- Documents `skf-test-skill`'s intentional alternative convention
- Preserves all load-bearing content (94 MANDATORY SEQUENCE headings + 52 CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE gates + Auto-Proceed inline instructions)

## What's still open on #335

- **C1** (EX distribution templates): demand-gated, no action needed until external user asks
- **C2** (DS resume after `[N]` cancel): needs its own design discussion issue
- **C4** (RS source-authority audit): demand-gated, no action needed until compliance adopter asks

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/.claude/projects/-home-armel-Projects-OSS-bmad-module-skill-forge/memory/feedback_verify_deferred_issue_framings.md",
  "content": "---\nname: verify-deferred-issue-framings\ndescription: Tracking issues that sit open accumulate stale claims; verify against current code before bulk-applying any cleanup the issue prescribes\nmetadata:\n  type: feedback\n---\n\nWhen closing out a deferred or parked tracking issue, verify every load-bearing claim in the issue body against current code state before executing the prescribed action. Issues that sit open accumulate staleness — claims that were true at filing time may have been superseded by intermediate work the issue didn't track.\n\n**Why:** Issue #335 (SKF Architecture/Utility/Management deferrals) framed the D1 trio cleanup as \"drop all three Workflow Rules bullets across 12 SKILL.md files.\" But the v2 skf-setup quality pass (commit 3dd864cd) had already made a deliberate **per-rule** decision: drop Rules 1+2 as restated baseline, *keep* Rule 3 for context-window management. The issue's framing predated that decision and would have undone it if bulk-applied. Two other stale claims in the same issue: D2's \"brief-skill is simpler auto-proceed style; update-skill is stricter\" (both carried the same pattern by the time the cleanup ran) and D2's \"drop three banner types\" (Auto-Proceed wasn't a banner — it was inline behavioral content).\n\n**How to apply:** Before any bulk action prescribed by a deferred issue:\n1. Re-grep the artifacts the issue references — counts, file lists, conventions\n2. Read the most recent commits that touched the relevant files (`git log -- <paths>`) to surface intermediate quality decisions\n3. Use claude-mem to search for prior reasoning about the artifacts\n4. Treat issue framings as hypotheses to verify, not specs to execute\n\nEspecially load-bearing when the issue uses words like \"drop everywhere,\" \"ratify everywhere,\" or \"whole-module decision\" — those framings are exactly where per-item nuance gets lost.\n\nRelated: [[project_skf_workflow_rules_convention]]\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/.claude/projects/-home-armel-Projects-OSS-bmad-module-skill-forge/memory/project_skf_workflow_rules_convention.md",
  "content": "---\nname: skf-workflow-rules-convention\ndescription: SKF Workflow Rules in SKILL.md use a per-rule convention — Rule 3 (no preload) is module-wide policy; Rules 1+2 were dropped as restated baseline in PR #336\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nSKF workflow skills carry a single bullet under `## Workflow Rules` in SKILL.md from the original \"trio\" pattern: **\"Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps.\"** This is module-wide policy as of PR #336 (commit 08c01c98 → merged to v2 as 811c8fff, 2026-05-16).\n\nThe previous \"trio\" (Read each step file completely + Follow the mandatory sequence exactly + Only load one step file at a time) was reduced to just Rule 3 because:\n- Rule 1 (\"read completely\") is restated baseline behavior for any LLM working from instructions\n- Rule 2 (\"follow sequence\") is restated by the `nextStepFile` chain mechanism + Stages table\n- Rule 3 (\"only load one\") is **not** restated elsewhere — it's the only rule that actively prevents an LLM failure mode (premature loading of future step files), which costs context budget and biases the model toward unnecessary content\n\n**Why:** The v2 skf-setup quality pass (commit 3dd864cd) introduced this per-rule decision. PR #336 propagated it to the 12 other workflow skills, fixed drift in `skf-quick-skill` (which was missing Rule 3 entirely from before the v2 pass), and documented `skf-test-skill`'s alternative step heading convention (`## STEP GOAL:` instead of `## MANDATORY SEQUENCE` — validation harness with score-driven semantics, not a generate-driven chain).\n\n**How to apply:**\n- New workflow skills authored after 2026-05-16 should carry only Rule 3 in Workflow Rules, plus any skill-specific rules\n- Do NOT add Rules 1 or 2 back — they're explicitly redundant\n- Do NOT remove Rule 3 — it's the load-bearing exception\n- `skf-forger` is the non-workflow exception (agent persona, no chain mechanics) — no Workflow Rules section at all\n- Step files preserve `## MANDATORY SEQUENCE` heading (structural) and `## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE` blocks (load-bearing step-transition gates) but should NOT carry the redundant `**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly...` ritual line\n\nRelated: [[feedback_verify_deferred_issue_framings]]\n"
}
```

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"Chain reachability lint for SKF workflow skills.\n\nFor each `src/skf-*/` workflow skill, asserts:\n\n1. Every step-file path listed in SKILL.md's Stages table exists on disk.\n2. Every `nextStepFile` value in step-file frontmatter resolves to an existing\n   file (or to a recognised external target such as `shared/health-check.md`).\n3. Every step file (anything with a `nextStepFile` frontmatter key, plus\n   anything explicitly named in the Stages table) is reachable from the\n   Stages-table entries by walking the `nextStepFile` chain.\n\nThe third check is the safety net: it catches step files that exist on disk\nbut no chain reaches — the failure mode the dropped \"Workflow Rules\" trio\nonly claimed to prevent. `skf-forger` is excluded because it is an agent\npersona, not a chained workflow.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport pathlib\nimport re\nfrom collections import deque\n\nimport pytest\n\nREPO_ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent\nSRC = REPO_ROOT / \"src\"\n\nWORKFLOW_SKILLS = sorted(\n    d.name\n    for d in SRC.iterdir()\n    if d.is_dir() and d.name.startswith(\"skf-\") and d.name != \"skf-forger\"\n)\n\n# `nextStepFile` values that resolve outside the skill directory.\n# `shared/health-check.md` resolves from the SKF module root (src/ in dev,\n# {project-root}/_bmad/skf/ when installed), per the comment in\n# `references/health-check.md` step files.\nEXTERNAL_TARGETS = {\"shared/health-check.md\"}\n\n\ndef _read_frontmatter(file_path: pathlib.Path) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the raw YAML frontmatter block (without the `---` fences), or None.\"\"\"\n    text = file_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    match = re.match(r\"^---\\n(.*?)\\n---\\n\", text, re.DOTALL)\n    return match.group(1) if match else None\n\n\ndef _next_step(file_path: pathlib.Path) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Extract the `nextStepFile` value from a step file's frontmatter.\"\"\"\n    fm = _read_frontmatter(file_path)\n    if fm is None:\n        return None\n    match = re.search(\n        r\"^nextStepFile:\\s*['\\\"]?([^'\\\"\\n]+?)['\\\"]?\\s*$\",\n        fm,\n        re.MULTILINE,\n    )\n    return match.group(1).strip() if match else None\n\n\ndef _stages_entries(skill_md: pathlib.Path) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Extract `references/...md` paths from the SKILL.md `## Stages` table.\"\"\"\n    text = skill_md.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    section = re.search(\n        r\"^## Stages\\b(.*?)(?=^## )\", text, flags=re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL\n    )\n    if not section:\n        return []\n    # Match `references/<anything>.md` cells; preserves sub/ paths.\n    return re.findall(r\"\\breferences/[A-Za-z0-9_./-]+\\.md\\b\", section.group(1))\n\n\ndef _resolve_next(current: pathlib.Path, next_value: str) -> pathlib.Path | None:\n    \"\"\"Resolve a nextStepFile value to an absolute path, or None for external targets.\"\"\"\n    if next_value in EXTERNAL_TARGETS:\n        return None\n    return (current.parent / next_value).resolve()\n\n\ndef _skill_inventory(skill: str) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Collect Stages entries, step files, and all references/*.md for one skill.\"\"\"\n    skill_dir = SRC / skill\n    skill_md = skill_dir / \"SKILL.md\"\n    ref_dir = skill_dir / \"references\"\n    if not skill_md.exists() or not ref_dir.exists():\n        return {}\n    all_md = sorted(p for p in ref_dir.rglob(\"*.md\"))\n    step_files = [p for p in all_md if _next_step(p) is not None]\n    return {\n        \"skill_dir\": skill_dir,\n        \"skill_md\": skill_md,\n        \"stages_entries\": _stages_entries(skill_md),\n        \"all_md\": all_md,\n        \"step_files\": step_files,\n    }\n\n\n@pytest.fixture(scope=\"module\")\ndef skills() -> dict[str, dict]:\n    return {skill: _skill_inventory(skill) for skill in WORKFLOW_SKILLS}\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"skill\", WORKFLOW_SKILLS)\ndef test_stages_entries_exist(skill: str, skills: dict[str, dict]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Stages-table entries must resolve to existing files under references/.\"\"\"\n    info = skills[skill]\n    if not info:\n        pytest.skip(f\"{skill}: no SKILL.md or references/\")\n    skill_dir = info[\"skill_dir\"]\n    missing = [\n        entry for entry in info[\"stages_entries\"] if not (skill_dir / entry).exists()\n    ]\n    assert not missing, (\n        f\"{skill}: SKILL.md Stages table references missing files: {missing}\"\n    )\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"skill\", WORKFLOW_SKILLS)\ndef test_next_step_files_resolve(skill: str, skills: dict[str, dict]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Every `nextStepFile` value must resolve to an existing file or external target.\"\"\"\n    info = skills[skill]\n    if not info:\n        pytest.skip(f\"{skill}: no SKILL.md or references/\")\n    broken: list[str] = []\n    for step in info[\"step_files\"]:\n        nx = _next_step(step)\n        if nx is None:\n            continue\n        if nx in EXTERNAL_TARGETS:\n            external = SRC / nx\n            if not external.exists():\n                broken.append(\n                    f\"{step.relative_to(info['skill_dir']).as_posix()} → {nx} \"\n                    f\"(external target missing at {external.as_posix()})\"\n                )\n            continue\n        resolved = _resolve_next(step, nx)\n        if resolved is not None and not resolved.exists():\n            broken.append(\n                f\"{step.relative_to(info['skill_dir']).as_posix()} → {nx} \"\n                f\"(resolves to {resolved.as_posix()})\"\n            )\n    assert not broken, f\"{skill}: broken nextStepFile references:\\n  \" + \"\\n  \".join(\n        broken\n    )\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"skill\", WORKFLOW_SKILLS)\ndef test_step_files_reachable_from_stages(\n    skill: str, skills: dict[str, dict]\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Every step file must be reachable from the Stages table via the nextStepFile chain.\"\"\"\n    info = skills[skill]\n    if not info:\n        pytest.skip(f\"{skill}: no SKILL.md or references/\")\n    skill_dir = info[\"skill_dir\"]\n\n    reachable: set[pathlib.Path] = set()\n    queue: deque[pathlib.Path] = deque()\n    for entry in info[\"stages_entries\"]:\n        path = (skill_dir / entry).resolve()\n        if path.exists() and path not in reachable:\n            reachable.add(path)\n            queue.append(path)\n\n    while queue:\n        current = queue.popleft()\n        nx = _next_step(current)\n        if nx is None or nx in EXTERNAL_TARGETS:\n            continue\n        next_path = _resolve_next(current, nx)\n        if next_path is None or not next_path.exists() or next_path in reachable:\n            continue\n        reachable.add(next_path)\n        queue.append(next_path)\n\n    orphans = [\n        step.relative_to(skill_dir).as_posix()\n        for step in info[\"step_files\"]\n        if step.resolve() not in reachable\n    ]\n === skf-quick-skill Stages table ===
```
```
## Stages

| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
|---|------|------|--------------|

| 2 | Ecosystem Check | references/ecosystem-check.md | Yes |
| 3 | Quick Extract | references/quick-extract.md | Yes |
| 4 | Compile | references/compile.md | No (review) |
| 5 | Write & Validate | references/write-and-validate.md | Yes |
| 6 | Finalize | references/finalize.md | Yes |
| 7 | Workflow Health Check | references/health-check.md | Yes |

## Invocation Contract

=== What's the first 30 lines of batch-mode.md? ===
---
nextStepFile: 'resolve-target.md'
---

<!-- Config: communicate in {communication_language}. -->

# Batch Mode

When `--batch <file>` is supplied, quick-skill processes a list of targets from a text file in sequence rather than a single target from arguments. Designed for unattended bulk runs (CI pipelines, mass-rebuilds, the skf-batch-skills meta-workflow when it lands).

## Input format

One target per line. Empty lines and lines starting with `#` (after optional leading whitespace) are ignored. Each non-empty line has the same shape as the single-target `target` argument, with optional space-separated per-line modifiers:

```
# A batch input file.
lodash
@vercel/og
cognee@0.5.0
https://github.com/foo/bar
https://github.com/foo/bar@2.1.0-beta

# Per-line modifiers — overrides for THIS target only:
lodash language=javascript scope=src/
cognee@0.5.0 language=python scope=cognee/api/
```

Recognised per-line modifiers:

| Modifier | Effect (this target only) |
```

> AGENT

This is an architecturally valid orphan, not a bug. `batch-mode.md` is a **conditional entry point** loaded from SKILL.md's On Activation section (when `--batch` is set), not the Stages table. The lint should treat any `references/...md` file mentioned anywhere in SKILL.md as a valid chain entry, not just Stages-table entries. Let me fix the test.

```json
  "old_string": "def _stages_entries(skill_md: pathlib.Path) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Extract `references/...md` paths from the SKILL.md `## Stages` table.\"\"\"\n    text = skill_md.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    section = re.search(\n        r\"^## Stages\\b(.*?)(?=^## )\", text, flags=re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL\n    )\n    if not section:\n        return []\n    # Match `references/<anything>.md` cells; preserves sub/ paths.\n    return re.findall(r\"\\breferences/[A-Za-z0-9_./-]+\\.md\\b\", section.group(1))",
  "new_string": "_REFERENCES_PATH_RE = re.compile(r\"\\breferences/[A-Za-z0-9_./-]+\\.md\\b\")\n\n\ndef _stages_entries(skill_md: pathlib.Path) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Extract `references/...md` paths from the SKILL.md `## Stages` table only.\"\"\"\n    text = skill_md.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    section = re.search(\n        r\"^## Stages\\b(.*?)(?=^## )\", text, flags=re.MULTILINE | re.DOTALL\n    )\n    if not section:\n        return []\n    return _REFERENCES_PATH_RE.findall(section.group(1))\n\n\ndef _skill_md_entries(skill_md: pathlib.Path) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Every `references/...md` path SKILL.md mentions — main flow entries plus\n    conditional entries invoked from On Activation (e.g. `--batch` modes).\"\"\"\n    text = skill_md.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    return list(dict.fromkeys(_REFERENCES_PATH_RE.findall(text)))"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/test/test-skf-chain-reachability.py",
  "old_string": "@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"skill\", WORKFLOW_SKILLS)\ndef test_step_files_reachable_from_stages(\n    skill: str, skills: dict[str, dict]\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Every step file must be reachable from the Stages table via the nextStepFile chain.\"\"\"\n    info = skills[skill]\n    if not info:\n        pytest.skip(f\"{skill}: no SKILL.md or references/\")\n    skill_dir = info[\"skill_dir\"]\n\n    reachable: set[pathlib.Path] = set()\n    queue: deque[pathlib.Path] = deque()\n    for entry in info[\"stages_entries\"]:\n        path = (skill_dir / entry).resolve()\n        if path.exists() and path not in reachable:\n            reachable.add(path)\n            queue.append(path)\n\n    while queue:\n        current = queue.popleft()\n        nx = _next_step(current)\n        if nx is None or nx in EXTERNAL_TARGETS:\n            continue\n        next_path = _resolve_next(current, nx)\n        if next_path is None or not next_path.exists() or next_path in reachable:\n            continue\n        reachable.add(next_path)\n        queue.append(next_path)\n\n    orphans = [\n        step.relative_to(skill_dir).as_posix()\n        for step in info[\"step_files\"]\n        if step.resolve() not in reachable\n    ]\n    assert not orphans, (\n        f\"{skill}: step files not reachable from Stages table chain: {orphans}\"\n    )",
  "new_string": "@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"skill\", WORKFLOW_SKILLS)\ndef test_step_files_reachable_from_skill_md(\n    skill: str, skills: dict[str, dict]\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Every step file must be reachable from a SKILL.md entry via the nextStepFile chain.\n\n    Entry set is every `references/...md` path SKILL.md mentions: Stages-table\n    rows plus conditional entries from On Activation (e.g. `--batch` modes).\n    \"\"\"\n    info = skills[skill]\n    if not info:\n        pytest.skip(f\"{skill}: no SKILL.md or references/\")\n    skill_dir = info[\"skill_dir\"]\n\n    reachable: set[pathlib.Path] = set()\n    queue: deque[pathlib.Path] = deque()\n    for entry in info[\"skill_md_entries\"]:\n        path = (skill_dir / entry).resolve()\n        if path.exists() and path not in reachable:\n            reachable.add(path)\n            queue.append(path)\n\n    while queue:\n        current = queue.popleft()\n        nx = _next_step(current)\n        if nx is None or nx in EXTERNAL_TARGETS:\n            continue\n        next_path = _resolve_next(current, nx)\n        if next_path is None or not next_path.exists() or next_path in reachable:\n            continue\n        reachable.add(next_path)\n        queue.append(next_path)\n\n    orphans = [\n        step.relative_to(skill_dir).as_posix()\n        for step in info[\"step_files\"]\n        if step.resolve() not in reachable\n    ]\n    assert not orphans, (\n        f\"{skill}: step files not reachable from SKILL.md entries: {orphans}\"\n    )"
}
```

> AGENT

**PR #337 opened against v2: https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/pull/337**

## What landed

- **New test**: `test/test-skf-chain-reachability.py` — 42 assertions across 14 workflow skills
- **Wired into**: `package.json` `test:python` suite (now 1388 tests, was 1346)
- **All pre-commit hooks pass**: schemas, install, CLI, workflow, validate:*, lint, lint:md, format:check

## The lint caught a real architectural insight while being built

Initial draft scoped chain entries to the Stages table only. That correctly flagged `skf-quick-skill/references/batch-mode.md` as an "orphan" — but the orphan wasn't a bug, it was a **conditional entry point** invoked from `On Activation` (when `--batch` is set) that re-enters the main chain via `nextStepFile: 'resolve-target.md'`. The test was refined to treat any `references/...md` mentioned anywhere in SKILL.md as a valid entry, which matches the actual execution semantics (the LLM loads any step file SKILL.md tells it to).

This pattern is now documented in the test's docstring and locked in by the test itself — future conditional entries follow the same shape and pass without modification.

## What's locked in

The chain integrity claim from PR #336 is now mechanically enforced:
- Rename a step file without updating its callsites → test fails
- Add a step file without wiring it into the chain from SKILL.md → test fails  
- Break a `nextStepFile` value → test fails

The dropped "Workflow Rules" trio said the LLM should follow the chain. This test says the chain must actually be intact.
